<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Gaussian moat conjecture: no infinite walk through distinct Gaussian primes can have bounded steps. More strongly, for each fixed finite step bound, the connected components of the Gaussian-prime graph have uniformly bounded size. This bound applies to every starting prime, including primes on the coordinate axes, and is nonexplicit. The proof constructs a finite periodic sieve obstruction using geometric sampling and information-theoretic estimates.

- **claim-uniform-prime-components** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-uniform)): For every finite real $D$ there is a finite $B_D$ such that every connected component of $G_D$ has at most $B_D$ vertices. The bound is independent of the starting prime and includes primes on the coordinate axes. Consequently every sequence of distinct Gaussian primes whose successive distances are at most $D$ has at most $B_D$ terms.
  - Explanation: Every Gaussian-prime component has uniformly bounded finite size for a fixed finite step bound, including components containing primes on the coordinate axes. The asserted bound is nonexplicit.
- **claim-finite-sieve-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-sieve)): For every $D\ge1$ there is a finite set $\mathcal P_D$ of rational primes congruent to $1$ modulo $4$ such that $\mathcal A(\mathcal P_D)$ contains no infinite sequence of distinct points with successive distances at most $D$. The set $\mathcal P_D$ depends only on $D$.
  - Explanation: A finite selection of split rational primes gives simultaneous congruence restrictions that exclude every infinite self-avoiding walk with the prescribed step bound. The selection depends only on that bound.
- **claim-periodic-component-bound** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-periodic)): Fix $D\ge1$ and a finite set $\mathcal P$ of rational primes congruent to $1$ modulo $4$. Suppose that $\mathcal A(\mathcal P)$ contains no infinite self-avoiding walk with steps at most $D$. Put $$Q=\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}p,\qquad K_D=\#\{v\in\mathbb Z[i]:\|v\|\le D\},$$ and let $E$ be the set of associates of the selected Gaussian factors. For every component $C$ of $G_D$, $$\#C\le\max\{Q^2,\ \|E\|+(K_D-1)\|E\|Q^2\}.$$
  - Explanation: A periodic avoidance obstruction gives a uniform bound for Gaussian-prime components after the associates of the selected factors are restored. The obstruction assumption is essential.
- **claim-displacement-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-displacement)): If $T_0,T_1$ are jointly distributed finite random times and $\|T_1-T_0\|\le n$ almost surely, then $$H(z_{T_1}-z_{T_0})\le2\log(n+1)+O_D(1).$$ The same bound holds conditionally on any event or variable under which the time-difference bound still holds. If $F$ is any finite vector of residue maps, then $$H(F(z_{T_1}))\ge H(F(z_{T_0}))-H(z_{T_1}-z_{T_0}).$$
  - Explanation: A bounded difference between sampled times restricts the displacement to a finite lattice disk. Its entropy bounds the possible loss of residue entropy under the displacement.
- **claim-conditional-entropy-continuity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-continuity)): If two laws of $(X,Y)$, on the same finite product alphabet, have total variation at most $\epsilon\le1/2$, and the product alphabet has at most $a$ elements, then $$\|H(X\mid Y)-H(\widetilde X\mid\widetilde Y)\| \le 2h(\epsilon)+2\epsilon\log a.$$
  - Explanation: Conditional entropies remain close when the joint laws are close in total variation and share a bounded finite alphabet.
- **claim-short-list-entropy-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-list)): Let $X$ have at most $a$ values, let $Y$ be finite data, and let $\mathcal L(Y)$ be a list of possible values of $X$. If $$\Pr\{X\in\mathcal L(Y),\ \|\mathcal L(Y)\|\le b\}=\rho, \qquad 1\le b\le a,$$ then $$\log a-H(X\mid Y)\ge \rho\log(a/b)-h(\rho).$$
  - Explanation: A short candidate list containing the true value with the stated probability forces a conditional entropy deficit. The success event need not be independent of the target value.
- **claim-many-segment-differences** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-differences)): Let $E$ consist of the points of a segment of $n\ge1$ steps of the walk. Let $R$ be its diameter, choose a line through two points realizing that diameter, and let $W$ be the larger of $1$ and the maximum distance of a point of $E$ from that line. Then, for $A=RW$, $$c n\le A\le D^2n^2, \qquad \|E-E\|\ge c_D A,$$ where $c>0$ is absolute and $c_D>0$ depends only on $D$.
  - Explanation: The enclosing rectangle of a self-avoiding walk segment has controlled area, and its area gives a lower bound for the number of distinct differences. The proof uses a surjective map between tori in the nondegenerate case.
- **claim-random-signed-rectangle-avoidance** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangle)): Let $p_1,\ldots,p_r$ be distinct rational primes congruent to $1$ modulo $4$, all in $[T,2T]$, where $T\ge5$, and put $P=\prod_i p_i$. Let $Q$ be an origin-centered rectangle of half-lengths $R_1\ge W_1\ge1$, in any orientation. Suppose $$R_1W_1\le P^{1-d},\qquad 0<d<\tfrac14.$$ There are absolute positive constants $c,C,C_0$ such that, if $d^3r\ge C_0$, independent sign choices satisfy $$\Pr\left\{Q\cap\Bigl(\prod_{i=1}^r\pi_i\Bigr)\mathbb Z[i] \ne\{0\}\right\} \le C e^{-c d^3r}.$$
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large collection of independent factor choices is unlikely to admit a nonzero product-lattice point in the specified rectangle. Determinant divisibility separates witness lines, and disjoint neighborhoods of sign classes combine the line estimates.
- **claim-geometric-entropy-enrichment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-enrichment)): Let $Z=z_\tau$ and $V$ be the starting point and sampled endpoint in the preceding transition for a fixed self-avoiding $D$-step walk. Set $$q=\lfloor U/L_*\rfloor,\qquad b=\lfloor g^2U/L_*\rfloor.$$ Consider any asymptotic family in which $D$ is fixed, $T\to\infty$, $q\le k$, and $$\frac{g^C U}{\log T}\longrightarrow\infty \quad\text{for every fixed }C>0.$$ Uniformly over the walks, batches, and starting-time laws, $$\label{eq:enrichment} \frac{f_V(b)}{bL_*} \ge \frac12\left(1+\frac{f_Z(q)}{qL_*}\right)-O_D(g).$$
  - Explanation: Sampling an endpoint of a uniformly selected distinct difference improves the normalized residue entropy. The estimate is asymptotic and uniform in the fixed walk, prime batch, and starting-time law.
- **claim-shared-conditioning-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditioning)): Let $(Y,\mathcal H)$ be any pair of finite variables, with $Y$ taking values in $\Lambda$, and let $S$ be selected independently as above. If $$\mathbb E_S H(F_S(Y))\ge(1-\epsilon)s\overline L, \qquad H(\mathcal H)\le\kappa s\overline L,$$ then $$\label{eq:deficit-upper} \mathop{\mathrm{av}}_c[\log p_c-H(\phi_c(Y)\mid\mathcal H)] \le(\epsilon+\kappa)\overline L.$$ No independence between $Y$ and $\mathcal H$ is required.
  - Explanation: The entropy cost of common auxiliary data is charged once against joint residue entropy before being averaged over coordinates. Independence of the target and the common data is unnecessary.
- **claim-one-step-coverage-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-transfer)): Use the signed coordinates and the experiment just defined, with integers $1\le s\le k$ and $n\ge1$. Let $$0<\tau\le3/8,\quad 0<\delta\le1/64,\quad \tau'=\tau+4\delta,\quad 0<\beta'\le\beta<1, \quad 0<\eta\le1,\quad 0\le\eta'\le1.$$ Suppose that at every $a$ in the support of $A$, all but a fraction at most $\eta'$ of the signed coordinates have $(\tau',\beta')$ coverage for $\phi_c(y(a)+h_1)$ under the law conditional on $A=a$. Assume also $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_S H(F_S(Y))&\ge(1-\epsilon)s\overline L, &H(\mathcal H)&\le\kappa s\overline L,\label{eq:transfer-entropy}\\ \eta'&\le\eta\delta/100, &\epsilon+\kappa&\le\eta\delta\beta'/16,\label{eq:transfer-budget} \end{aligned}$$ where $\epsilon,\kappa\ge0$. Finally, for every coordinate size $p=p_j$, assume $$\begin{aligned} p\exp(-\delta^2np^{-\beta}/2)&\le\delta/100, &\delta^{-1}&<p^{\beta'/2},\label{eq:transfer-size}\\ \log(2e/\delta)&\le(\beta'/4)\log T.\label{eq:transfer-log} \end{aligned}$$ Then all but a fraction at most $\eta$ of the coordinates have $(\tau,\beta)$ coverage for $\phi_c(Z)$.
  - Explanation: Conditional coverage at every exact mixing value transfers to coverage of the terminal mixture when the joint entropy, shared-data cost, trial count, and coordinate-size inequalities all hold. The transfer permits exceptional residues and exceptional coordinates.
- **claim-future-shifts-and-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-future-shift)): Let $V$ be a scheduled scale in a band with parameter $g$. For all sufficiently large $M$, the maximum total forward time shift from the checkpoint before $V$ to any later checkpoint, including $t_*$, is bounded by a deterministic number $S_V$ satisfying $$\log(1+S_V)\le2gV.$$ Consequently the entropy of its physical displacement is $O_D(gV)$, for every starting law. Furthermore, conditional on any earlier exact checkpoint start, $$\label{eq:smoothing} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal L(t_*),\mathcal L(t_*+a)\bigr) \le T_+^{-9}\qquad (0\le a\le T_+,\ a\in\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: The remaining schedule has a deterministic advance bound, which controls physical displacement entropy. A final independent uniform offset makes the common terminal-time law nearly invariant under the permitted integer shifts.
- **claim-terminal-residue-coverage** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-coverage)): For all sufficiently large $M$, uniformly over the batches, $0\le i\le J$, and all exact start times $a\ge0$ at $A_i$, all but a fraction at most $\eta_i$ of the signed primes satisfy $$\label{eq:coverage} \#\{u\in\mathbb Z[i]/(\pi):b_{i,a,\pi}(u)<\tau_i/p\} <p^{1-\beta_i}.$$
  - Explanation: Backward induction establishes terminal coverage from every exact start at every specified checkpoint. The conclusion allows both a fraction of failing signed primes and an exceptional set of residues for each remaining prime.
- **claim-batch-information-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-information-rate)): There is a constant $c_1>0$, depending only on the fixed schedule parameters, such that for all sufficiently large $M$ and every batch in <a href="#eq:batches" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:batches">[eq:batches]</a>, $$\frac{1}{L_T}\, \mathbb E_{\mathcal C} I\bigl(X'_T;W_{L_T}(t_*)\mid O'_T\bigr) \ge \frac{c_1}{\log T}. \label{eq:information-rate}$$ The threshold for $M$ is uniform over all infinite self-avoiding $D$-step walks in $\mathcal A(\mathcal P_M)$.
  - Explanation: Under simultaneous zero avoidance, a short terminal increment word carries a positive conditional information rate about its selected batch residues after the smaller batch residues are known. The rate is averaged over auxiliary prime choices.
- **claim-common-law-entropy-telescope** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-telescope)): For every fixed collection $\mathcal C$ of prime choices, $$\sum_T\frac{I(X'_T;W_{L_T}(t_*)\mid O'_T)}{L_T} \le\log K_D+o(1), \label{eq:information-telescope}$$ where the error tends to zero uniformly over the choices and the walks as $M\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Nested residue vectors and compatible increment-word lengths make the information charges telescope under the common smoothed time law. The total charge is bounded by the entropy available in a bounded increment, up to a uniform vanishing error.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Gaussian-prime graph** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite real $D$, let $G_D$ have all Gaussian primes as vertices, with an edge between distinct $z,w$ if $\|z-w\|\le D$.
- **Periodic avoidance set** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a finite set $\mathcal P$ of such rational primes, put $$\mathcal A(\mathcal P)=\{z\in\mathbb Z[i]: z\not\equiv0\pmod{\pi_p},\quad z\not\equiv0\pmod{\overline{\pi_p}}\quad(p\in\mathcal P)\}.$$
- **Split-prime batch and logarithmic mean** ([source](#eq:prime-count)): For $T>1$, let $$\mathcal B_T=\{p\text{ rational prime}:T\le p\le2T,\ p\equiv1\pmod4\}, \qquad k_T=\|\mathcal B_T\|, \qquad L_T^*=\frac1{k_T}\sum_{p\in\mathcal B_T}\log p.$$
- **Finite-variable entropy** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a finite variable $X$ with law $p$, $H(X)=-\sum_xp(x)\log p(x)$, with $0\log0=0$.
- **Random time** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A random time is a finitely supported nonnegative integer-valued variable.
- **Signed subset** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A signed subset $\mathcal S$ consists of a subset of distinct primes from $\mathcal P$, with one of the two conjugate Gaussian factors chosen for each.
- **Averaged residue entropy** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a finitely supported point variable $Z\in\mathbb Z[i]$, define $$F_{\mathcal S}(Z)=(Z\bmod\pi)_{\pi\in\mathcal S}, \qquad f_Z(s)=\mathbb E_{\mathcal S}H(F_{\mathcal S}(Z)), \quad 0\le s\le k,$$
- **Residue coverage** ([source](#eq:coverage-definition)): For a variable $X$ in an alphabet of size $p$, say explicitly that it has $(\tau,\beta)$ coverage when $$\label{eq:coverage-definition} \#\{u:\mathbb P(X=u)<\tau/p\}<p^{1-\beta}.$$
- **Checkpoint and exact-start continuation** ([source](#pm-h-26)): A *checkpoint* is a stage just before a specified transition, or the terminal stage.
- **Increment word** ([source](#sec:information)): For a nonnegative integer time $t$, write $$W_L(t)=(z_{t+1}-z_t,\ldots,z_{t+L}-z_{t+L-1})$$ for the next $L$ increments.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-periodic-prime-component-cap | \#C | ≤ | $\max\{Q^2,\ \|E\|+(K_D-1)\|E\|Q^2\}$ | The period, increment count, and exceptional associates are those defined in the proposition. |
| q-split-prime-batch-count | k_T | = | $(1/2+o(1))\frac{T}{\log T}$ | The interval parameter tends to infinity with the modulus fixed. |
| q-mean-prime-log-upper | L_T^* | ≤ | $\log(2T)$ | The average ranges over the primes in the defined batch. |
| q-displacement-entropy | H(z_{T_1}-z_{T_0}) | ≤ | $2\log(n+1)+O_D(1)$ | The step bound is fixed; the additive constant depends only on it. |
| q-conditional-entropy-continuity | Conditional entropy difference | ≤ | $2h(\epsilon)+2\epsilon\log a$ | The total variation tolerance and alphabet bound satisfy the statement's restrictions. |
| q-short-list-deficit | \log a-H(X\mid Y) | ≥ | $\rho\log(a/b)-h(\rho)$ | The success probability and list-size bound are those of the stated event. |
| q-segment-rectangle-area | A | ≤ | $D^2n^2$ | The segment has the positive number of steps specified in the lemma. |
| q-distinct-difference-count | \|E-E\| | ≥ | $c_D A$ | The positive prefactor depends only on the fixed step bound. |
| q-signed-rectangle-failure | Probability of a nonzero product-lattice point in the rectangle | ≤ | $C e^{-c d^3r}$ | The constants are absolute; the geometric and size conditions must all hold. |
| q-entropy-enrichment-fraction | \frac{f_V(b)}{bL_*} | ≥ | $\frac12\left(1+\frac{f_Z(q)}{qL_*}\right)-O_D(g)$ | The walk step bound is fixed and all the stated asymptotic growth conditions apply. |
| q-shared-data-conditional-deficit | Average coordinate conditional entropy deficit | ≤ | $(\epsilon+\kappa)\overline L$ | The common data may depend on the lattice-valued target. |
| q-remaining-time-logarithm | \log(1+S_V) | ≤ | $2gV$ | The scale and transition parameter belong to the specified band. |
| q-terminal-time-smoothing | Total variation of the terminal time law and its translate | ≤ | $T_+^{-9}$ | Only the stated nonnegative integer translates are covered. |
| q-baseline-coverage-exponent | \beta_0 | = | $\frac1{200}$ | Chosen before the asymptotic parameter and independently of the walk. |
| q-baseline-entropy-error | e_0 | = | $\frac{\beta_0}{50}$ | Defined from the baseline coverage exponent before choosing the large scales. |
| q-checkpoint-coverage-exceptions | Number of terminal residues below the coverage floor | < | $p^{1-\beta_i}$ | The floor and exceptional fraction are the checkpoint parameters. |
| q-information-cost-per-increment | Average conditional information per increment for a batch | ≥ | $\frac{c_1}{\log T}$ | The positive constant depends only on the fixed schedule parameters. |
| q-common-law-information-budget | Summed conditional information rates over batches | ≤ | $\log K_D+o(1)$ | The error vanishes uniformly over prime choices and walks. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-displacement-counting** [claim](#stmt-lem-displacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-displacement): Count possible lattice displacements in a bounded disk and apply the entropy chain rule to residue translation. -> Displacement entropy bound and residue entropy preservation inequality.
- **step-entropy-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-continuity): Couple the joint laws and apply entropy continuity to the joint variables and conditioning marginals. -> A conditional entropy continuity estimate.
- **step-short-list-deficit** [claim](#stmt-lem-list) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-list): Introduce the list-success indicator and bound conditional support separately on success and failure. -> An entropy deficit forced by short-list recovery.
- **step-many-differences** [claim](#stmt-lem-differences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-differences): Use lattice packing to control the diameter rectangle and a nonzero-degree torus map to obtain a covering by projected difference disks. -> Area bounds and a lower bound on distinct differences.
- **step-random-factor-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-rectangle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rectangle): Combine determinant divisibility for nearby sign choices, concentration for a fixed witness line, and disjoint enlargements of line classes in the Boolean cube. -> A small probability of a nonzero product-lattice witness.
- **step-enrich-endpoint-entropy** [claim](#stmt-lem-enrichment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-enrichment): Separate uniformly sampled differences in fresh residue coordinates, then use conditional entropy and concavity to retain starting entropy in a randomly chosen endpoint. -> The normalized endpoint entropy enrichment inequality.
- **step-charge-common-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditioning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditioning): Subtract the entropy of shared data once from joint entropy and average conditional subadditivity over independently selected signed subsets. -> An average conditional coordinate deficit bound.
- **step-transfer-coverage** [claim](#stmt-thm-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-transfer): Assume too many deficient coordinates, construct short candidate lists from common displacement trials, and contradict the shared conditional deficit bound. -> Coverage for the terminal mixture outside the permitted fraction of coordinates.
- **step-control-future-schedule** [claim](#stmt-lem-future-shift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-future-shift): Bound all remaining transition advances by a deterministic sum and use the final independent uniform offset to control translated terminal laws. -> Remaining-time and displacement bounds together with terminal smoothing.
- **step-backward-checkpoint-coverage** [claim](#stmt-prop-coverage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-coverage): Iterate and preserve entropy through the schedule, establish the terminal marginal base using relative entropy, then verify the coverage-transfer hypotheses at each earlier checkpoint. -> Uniform terminal coverage from every exact checkpoint start.
- **step-charge-zero-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-prop-information-rate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-information-rate): Use posterior mixtures of exact-start continuations to test candidate residues, shrink passing lists with independent packages, and transport the resulting information to terminal increment words. -> A positive conditional information rate for each batch.
- **step-telescope-information** [claim](#stmt-lem-telescope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-telescope): Partition longer increment words into shorter compatible blocks, update nested residue vectors by preceding increments, and compare block laws using terminal smoothing and entropy continuity. -> A uniform upper bound on summed information rates.
- **step-existential-finite-sieve** [claim](#stmt-thm-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-telescope-2): Average the information telescope over prime choices and contradict it with the divergent total of batch information lower bounds, then choose a sufficiently large scale uniformly over avoiding walks. -> A finite split-prime selection excluding every infinite avoiding walk with the fixed step bound.
- **step-periodic-component-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-periodic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-periodic): Use local finiteness to obtain an infinite simple path from an infinite component, inject finite components into residue classes by translation invariance, and restore the finitely many exceptional prime associates. -> The conditional uniform Gaussian-prime component bound.
- **step-uniform-moat-conclusion** [claim](#stmt-cor-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-periodic): Apply the periodic component reduction to the finite sieve obstruction and use lattice separation for step bounds below the lattice spacing. -> A uniform finite bound for every Gaussian-prime component and every distinct-prime walk with the fixed step bound.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniform-component-finiteness**: A fixed finite step bound gives a uniform finite bound on Gaussian-prime component cardinalities, including components containing primes on the coordinate axes.
- **invariant-periodic-reduction-is-conditional**: The periodic component bound requires an avoiding graph with no infinite self-avoiding walk; periodicity then gives an injection of each avoiding component into the finite residue torus. Restoring the selected prime associates requires the additional contribution in the stated bound.
- **invariant-shared-conditioning-cost**: A common conditioning vector incurs its entropy cost in the joint entropy budget before that cost is averaged over residue coordinates. Independence between the point variable and the conditioning vector is unnecessary.
- **invariant-coverage-allows-exceptions**: Coverage supplies a probability floor outside a strictly bounded exceptional set. It does not assert that every residue has that floor, and the scheduled coverage conclusion also permits an exceptional fraction of signed primes.
- **invariant-common-terminal-law-budget**: The information charges are summed under a common terminal-time law, with nested residue vectors and compatible word lengths. Approximate translation invariance supplies the comparison between shifted blocks; stationarity of the walk is unnecessary.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-explicit-resource-budget**: Choose a hard timeout, storage allocation, or traversal cutoff from the asserted component bound. -> The proof supplies no explicit numerical component bound or sufficiently large schedule threshold from which such a resource budget can be obtained.
- **anti-use-repeated-vertex-length-bound**: Bound the duration of a walk that may revisit Gaussian primes by the component cardinality bound. -> A finite component cardinality does not bound the number of visits when vertices may repeat.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-locally-finite-graphs**: Infer a uniform component-size bound for an arbitrary locally finite graph from the absence of an infinite simple path. -> Finite components can have unbounded sizes across the graph; the periodic injection used here is an additional ingredient.
- **anti-use-unverified-sieve-selection**: Insert an arbitrary finite set of split primes into the periodic formula and regard the result as a certified component bound. -> Finiteness and splitting of the selected primes alone do not establish the absence of an infinite avoiding walk.
- **anti-use-unconditional-continuation-independence**: Treat repeated continuations as independent after averaging over their exact checkpoint start. -> Mixing over a shared checkpoint can preserve dependence between continuations and their starting position, invalidating an unconditional concentration argument.
- **anti-use-complete-residue-coverage**: Replace the coverage estimate by a positive probability floor at every residue for every signed prime. -> The coverage definition allows exceptional residues, and the scheduled proposition allows exceptional signed primes.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal conclusion is an existential uniform component bound, obtained from a finite sieve whose scale is chosen through asymptotic estimates and contradiction. The source states: “The bound $B_D$ is nonexplicit: the proof chooses sufficiently large scales after fixing $D$.” The finite sieve theorem asserts existence of a suitable selection depending only on the step bound; the supplied proof gives no explicit numerical selection threshold for an executable certificate of the principal conclusion. Finite experiments with selected walk segments or residue classes would therefore leave the uniform theorem undecided.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform component theorem supplies a mathematical termination guarantee for exhaustive traversal of a Gaussian-prime component, but its nonexplicit bound supplies no concrete software resource contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-length conclusion concerns sequences of distinct Gaussian primes. It does not bound the duration of a simulation that revisits positions. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The periodic reduction requires an already established obstruction to infinite self-avoiding walks. A finite periodic filter alone does not satisfy that prerequisite or provide a completed obstruction-checking construction. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The forward transition explicitly constructs a sampler from a finite self-avoiding lattice segment: distinct differences receive equal weight, a deterministic representative pair is selected, and an independent fair endpoint choice produces the output. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The component injection into a finite residue torus is a cardinality argument. It does not specify a representation supporting data-structure operations or their costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geometric separation estimate controls collisions under random Gaussian-factor choices. It supplies no hardware mapping or accelerator construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The independent fair signs select conjugate factors for an entropy argument. They are not accompanied by a cryptographic construction, adversarial model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Entropy enrichment concerns residue projections of a fixed deterministic walk. The paper assigns no probability law to the walk itself and provides no learning construction. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The final sieve selection depends on sufficiently large scales chosen uniformly over hypothetical avoiding walks. No explicit optimisation objective or constructive parameter-selection method is supplied. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The shared-data lemma amortises conditioning entropy across residue coordinates. It does not construct a communication protocol, and its auxiliary coordinate selection must remain independent of the measured variables. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The broader foundational contribution proves a uniform bound for the specified Gaussian-prime graph. Its uniformity cannot be transferred to arbitrary locally finite graphs solely from the absence of infinite paths. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-finite-step-bound** (hypothesis): The uniform component conclusion requires a single finite step bound for the entire graph. It does not establish uniform component bounds when permitted steps grow without a common finite bound. Blocked use: ['Applying the conclusion to position-dependent step allowances without establishing a common finite bound.']
- **limit-distinct-vertex-length-conclusion** (hypothesis): The bound on sequence length requires distinct Gaussian primes. Component finiteness bounds distinct vertices, but does not bound the duration of a walk that revisits vertices. Blocked use: ['Using the sequence-length conclusion as a termination bound for a simulation permitting repeated visits.']
- **limit-nonexplicit-uniform-bound** (nonconstructive): The uniform component bound is nonexplicit because the proof chooses sufficiently large scales after fixing the step bound. Blocked use: ['Deriving numerical buffer capacities, traversal cutoffs, or hard execution deadlines directly from the uniform bound.']
- **limit-periodic-obstruction-premise** (hypothesis): The periodic component formula requires the selected avoidance set to contain no infinite self-avoiding walk with the prescribed step bound. A finite selection of split primes alone does not establish this premise. Blocked use: ['Treating an arbitrary finite selection of split primes as a certified obstruction.']
- **limit-periodicity-for-uniformity** (formal_scope): The passage from absence of infinite paths to a uniform component bound uses periodicity. The same implication is unavailable for general locally finite graphs. Blocked use: ['Transferring the uniform component conclusion to arbitrary locally finite graphs solely because they lack infinite simple paths.']
- **limit-exceptional-prime-associates** (edge_case): The avoiding-graph component cap does not by itself account for Gaussian primes divisible by selected factors. Their associates must be restored with the additional contribution in the periodic proposition. Blocked use: ['Applying the avoiding-component cap directly to every Gaussian-prime component while omitting selected-factor associates.']
- **limit-finite-time-displacement-control** (hypothesis): The displacement entropy estimate requires finite random times with the stated almost-sure bound on their difference. Its conditional version retains that requirement under the conditioning. Blocked use: ['Replacing the almost-sure time-difference bound by an expected bound without a separate entropy argument.']
- **limit-distinct-difference-sampling** (hypothesis): The endpoint transition samples distinct differences uniformly, chooses a representative ordered pair by a fixed deterministic rule, and then uses an independent fair endpoint choice. Uniform sampling of ordered pairs generally defines a different transition. Blocked use: ['Substituting uniform ordered-pair sampling into the entropy enrichment proof without establishing the required difference law.']
- **limit-signed-rectangle-regime** (hypothesis): The random rectangle estimate concerns distinct split rational primes in the prescribed interval, a rectangle satisfying the stated area restriction, and independent factor choices under the size threshold. It does not assert deterministic avoidance for every choice of signs. Blocked use: ['Using the probability estimate as a guarantee for an arbitrary fixed factor selection.', 'Dropping the rectangle area or factor-count restrictions.']
- **limit-enrichment-asymptotic-regime** (approximation): Entropy enrichment holds in the specified asymptotic family with fixed step bound and the stated growth condition for every fixed exponent. It supplies no unconditional finite-parameter accuracy guarantee. Blocked use: ['Assigning a numerical entropy error to a small finite segment without controlling the asymptotic remainder and scale conditions.']
- **limit-fixed-map-entropy-averaging** (hypothesis): Residue maps are fixed before entropy is computed, and those entropies are then averaged over map choices. Entropy of a mixture including the choices is a different quantity. Blocked use: ['Replacing averaged fixed-map entropies by the entropy of a mixture that includes map randomness.']
- **limit-shared-data-and-conditional-trials** (hypothesis): Coverage transfer uses displacement trials independent conditional on the exact mixing variable, together with an unconditional entropy budget for their shared data vector. Independence after mixing is not supplied, and separate conditional entropy bounds do not replace the shared unconditional budget. Blocked use: ['Applying concentration to trials as though they were independent after averaging over their common checkpoint.', 'Replacing the unconditional shared-data entropy budget by bounds conditional on exact starts.']
- **limit-coverage-retains-exceptions** (formal_scope): Coverage permits exceptional residues, and scheduled terminal coverage also permits an exceptional fraction of signed primes. The exceptional factors may depend on the exact start; the proposition does not select a single universally covered subset. Blocked use: ['Claiming a positive probability floor at every residue for every selected factor.', 'Interchanging the exact-start and covered-factor quantifiers to obtain a common covered subset.']
- **limit-zero-avoidance-information-cost** (hypothesis): The positive batch information cost requires simultaneous zero avoidance for the selected factors. The candidate-testing proof also uses self-avoidance and short words to make the hit events disjoint. Blocked use: ['Applying the information-rate lower bound to an unrestricted bounded-step walk.', 'Adding hit probabilities for a word without proving that distinct offsets cannot hit the same residue.']
- **limit-common-law-entropy-telescope** (approximation): The information telescope uses nested residue vectors, compatible dyadic word lengths, and the common smoothed terminal-time law. Its error vanishes asymptotically; the proof does not assume stationarity of the deterministic walk. Blocked use: ['Summing independently obtained batch charges at unrelated time laws.', 'Discarding the continuity error at finite schedule size.', 'Partitioning longer words into incompatible shorter lengths without a replacement argument.']
- **limit-existential-sieve-selection** (nonconstructive): The final finite sieve is selected after a contradiction at sufficiently large scales, uniformly over hypothetical avoiding walks. This establishes existence but supplies no explicit numerical selection threshold for the principal theorem. Blocked use: ['Presenting a finite experimental sieve or sampled segment as a certificate of the full uniform theorem.', 'Claiming that the proof specifies an explicit executable parameter selection for the principal obstruction.']

Scope note of family 028 (`lean/docs/028.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Bounded-Step Walks on Gaussian Primes](../../preprints/Bounded-Step-Walks-on-Gaussian-Primes-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Gaussian moat problem asks whether an infinite walk through distinct Gaussian primes can have bounded step lengths. The formalized result gives a negative answer for every real step bound $D$. More strongly, one finite bound depending only on $D$ limits the size of every connected component of the bounded-step graph and the length of every injective bounded-step walk. Axis primes and all associates are included. No explicit function of $D$ is supplied.

Comparator source `GaussianMoat.lean` (1166 bytes, sha256 `f1d2bdcb38ababb65d98dba286dfe13286c1709aea704d9dac58e2004ca80e84`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace GaussianMoat

abbrev PrimeVertex := {z : GaussianInt // Irreducible z}

noncomputable def primeGraph (D : ℝ) : SimpleGraph PrimeVertex where
  Adj p q := p ≠ q ∧ dist (p.val : ℂ) (q.val : ℂ) ≤ D
  symm := by
    constructor
    intro p q h
    exact ⟨h.1.symm, by simpa only [dist_comm] using h.2⟩
  loopless := by
    constructor
    intro p h
    exact h.1 rfl

def component (D : ℝ) (p : PrimeVertex) : Set PrimeVertex :=
  {q | (primeGraph D).Reachable p q}

def MainEndpoint : Prop :=
  ∀ (D : ℝ) (z : ℕ → GaussianInt),
    (∀ t, Irreducible (z t)) → Function.Injective z →
    (∀ t, dist (z (t + 1) : ℂ) (z t : ℂ) ≤ D) → False

def UniformEndpoint : Prop :=
  ∀ D : ℝ, ∃ B : ℕ,
    (∀ p : PrimeVertex, (component D p).Finite ∧ (component D p).ncard ≤ B) ∧
    (∀ (n : ℕ) (z : Fin n → GaussianInt),
      (∀ i, Irreducible (z i)) → Function.Injective z →
      (∀ i j : Fin n, j.val = i.val + 1 → dist (z j : ℂ) (z i : ℂ) ≤ D) →
      n ≤ B)

theorem fullMain : MainEndpoint ∧ UniformEndpoint := by
  sorry

end GaussianMoat

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Gaussian moat conjecture: no infinite walk through distinct Gaussian primes can have bounded steps. More strongly, for each fixed finite step bound, the connected components of the Gaussian-prime graph have uniformly bounded size. This bound applies to every starting prime, including primes on the coordinate axes, and is nonexplicit. The proof constructs a finite periodic sieve obstruction using geometric sampling and information-theoretic estimates.
